# Sefer HaYirah

ספר היראה

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah)

רבנים והוגים שחיו במאה ה-11 ועד המאה ה-15.

Short 13th-century essay attributed to Rabbeinu Yonah Gerondi describing how one should act from the time they wake up in the morning and throughout the day.

Sections: Paragraph

Location: Musar/Rishonim

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Sefer HaYirah, Vilna 1886 (Segments: 331, License: unknown)

Version (En): Sefer ha-Yirah, trans. Rabbi Justin S. Kerber, 2007 (Segments: 331)


## Paragraph 1

###### Sefer HaYirah 1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/1)


להורות לעם ה׳ את דרך ה׳. ואת המעשה אשר יעשון כל ימיהם. בשבתם ובקומם. בלכתם ובבואם. למען ייטב להם ולבניהם. בזה ובבא.

טוב לגבר כי ישא עול בנעוריו (איכה ג). כי טוב לאדם לשאת ולסבול עולו של הקב"ה ולתת מוסרות ומוטות על צוארו להכנס לעבודת בורא העולם.


*Sefer ha-Yirah,* the Book of Awe, of our Pious Rabbi Yonah Gerondi of blessed memory. To show the people of God the way of God and the deeds which they may do all of their days, when they lie down and when they rise up, when they go and when they come, so that it may be good for them and their children, in this [world] and in [the world-to-] come. ¹

I.² **It is good** that a man³ should take up the Yoke [of the commandments] in his youth. (Lam. 3:27) For it is good that a person⁴ should take up and to endure the Yoke of the Holy One Blessed Be He⁵ and to set the reins and the staffs⁶ upon his neck to enter into the service of the Creator of the World.

footnotes:
¹ Superscription in Me’ah She’arim edition. Vilna edition reads: “That was made by our completely pious Rabbi Yonah Gerondi, may his memory be for a blessing.” Moriah edition reads, “of the Genius, the pious Rabbenu Yonah of Gerondi, may his memory be for a blessing.”
² The Arabic-numeral line numbers follow the edition of מקור היראה, by R. Benjamin Moshe Zilber (Zilber ed.). The Roman-numeral section numbers follow the edition containing the gloss of R. Moshe Negrin (Negrin ed.).
³ גבר
⁴ אדם
⁵ I have chosen not to use gender-sensitive translation. The book is gender-specific and the translation will try to be true to its setting in time and place.
⁶ Zilber ed. omits ומותות.



## Paragraph 2

###### Sefer HaYirah 2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/2)


ויזכור את בוראו בימי (כחו) [בחורותיו] (כצ"ל. ומלשון הכתוב (קהלת יב). וזכור את בוראיך בימי בחורותיך)


Now he should remember his Creator in the days of his youth.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ Zilber, Moriah read “כחו,” “his strength.” Mea She’arim reads בחורותיו, “his youth.” Vilna gives כחו in round brackets, בחורותיו in square brackets, and resolves the variation thus, “and so it must be read. From the written language, Ecc. 12:1. “Remember your creator in the days of your youth.”Makor ha-Yirah, “I have learned in Duties of the Heart (R. Bachya Ibn Pakuda) which explains that the distinction between today and tomorrow is the same difference as between youth and age, and this is the language in the chapter on making personal accounting (חשבון הנפש) #13 therefore put yourself to this length as much as you are able to pay what you owe to the Creator every day (which means every day you remain (lit., lengthen) upon this earth one should pay back, relieve, and complete today what you owe to the creator, etc.) and do not put off to tomorrow what can be done today lest it should be weighty for you to separate (i.e., die) if you arrive tomorrow and it shall be hard from this if it shall come to your end and closed off for you and your error shall end for the world is like a market that will be gathered and afterward separated and he that pays the reward is happy and he that causes loss feels sorry for it therefore the wise one (Solomon) says “remember your creator…” Thus far his words. [Zilber goes on to cite R. Yonah’s own work, The Gates of Repentance, II:32, “...when one defers repentance, his transgressions age and lose their sting and he does not worry over them as in the beginning.” (The Gates of Repentance, trans. Shraga Silverstein. Feldheim: New York, 1967. 73.)

Each and every day he should strengthen and be encouraged in the commandments of the Creator of the World,



## Paragraph 4

###### Sefer HaYirah 4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/4)


ויכניס אהבתו בלבו


he should bring His love into his heart⁸ that to bring love into one’s heart means to act like Abraham in his great love for his fellowmen. He also discusses the commentators on whether love of God and fear of God are one or two separate *mitzvot*. and

footnotes:
⁸ There are several 3ms pronouns in a row here – the translation tries to keep which refer to God and which refer to man straight by use of capitalization.Makor ha-Yirah: a long exigesis arguing on the basis of Rambam sefer mitzvoth gedolot



## Paragraph 5

###### Sefer HaYirah 5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/5)


(ויחשוב). [אולי צ"ל וישווה וכמשה"כ (תהלים יז) שויתי ה' לנגדי תמיד] הבורא תמיד לנגד עיניו. ועל ידי כן יתן יראת הצור על פניו.


he should think⁹ of his Creator [as] eternally before his [very] eyes. And thus he may set the fear¹⁰ of the Rock [of Israel] upon his face, and

footnotes:
⁹ Vilna places (ויחשוב) in round brackets. In square brackets it adds, “Perhaps it should read “set before,” as the verse says, (Ps. 16:8) ‘I have set the ETERNAL before me always.’”
¹⁰ The first occurrence of the word “יִרְאָה” . There are several translation options available: fear, awe, or reverence. "Awe" and "reverence" would both be legitimate choices, and would sound more euphonious to a modern audience, especially one familiar with Abraham Joshua Heschel and his idea that radical amazement is the foundation of faith. But "fear" seems more in keeping with Rabbeinu Yonah's medieval sensibilities and closer to what he actually meant.

make his heart tremble,

and always crying over them.¹¹

¹¹ Although pietist, this is not a chassidic spirituality. This halachah stands in sharp contrast to the teachings of Rebbe Nachman of Breslov, who would say, מצוה גדולה היא להיות בשמחה.



## Paragraph 10

###### Sefer HaYirah 10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/10)


וישמור בכל כחו מחטוא עוד במסתרים. ולא יעשה במחשך מעשיו. ואז יהיה אהוב ונחמד בעיני הבורא. וכל רואיו יאשרוהו:


So let him keep with all his strength from sinning more in secret, and let him not do it in dark deeds. And so he will be beloved and treasured in the eyes of the Creator and all who see him will rejoice in him.



## Paragraph 11

###### Sefer HaYirah 11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/11)


אלה הדברים אשר יעשה אותם האדם וחי בהם חיי העולם. בכל בקר בהקיצו משנתו יזדעזע ויהיה נרתע ונחפר מאימת הבורא. בזכרו חסדו אשר עשה לו ואמונתו אשר שמר לו. כי החזיר לו נשמתו אשר הפקיד אצלו


II. **These are the things that a man may do and live life everlasting by them**.¹² Every morning when he awakens from his sleep, let him tremble and be startled and seized by the fear of the Creator when he remembers the loving kindness¹³ by which He did for him and the faith which He kept by him, for He returned his soul which He entrusted to him.

footnotes:
¹² Boldface in Moriah edition.
¹³ חסד. A notoriously difficult word to translate; it could mean "love," "grace," "kindness," or "charity."



## Paragraph 12

###### Sefer HaYirah 12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/12)


ואז יברך בלבו הבורא אשר הגדיל לעשות עמו כי חדש והחליף כחו. ובשומו הדברים האלה על לבו תבער אהבת הבורא בלבו.


So then with [all] his heart let him bless the Creator who has done great things¹⁴ with him, for He has renewed and restored his strength. And when he sets these words upon his heart¹⁵ the love of the Creator will burn in his heart.

footnotes:
¹⁴ הגדיל לעשות. See Ps. 126:2-3 for similar usage.
¹⁵ The language echoes the ואהבת prayer.



## Paragraph 13

###### Sefer HaYirah 13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/13)


ואז אל (ישב מטתו דרך עצל) [ישכב במטתו כדרך העצל] אך במהירות וזריזות יקום מיד. ואם יפגע בו מנוול ויוליכנו דרך עצלות. ישיב אמריו לו. וישים אל לבו. כי אם יקרא בעל חוב או אדם אחר אליו יקום מיד. או מפני כבוד אדם. או מפני ריוח (שיבא) [שיביא] לו חיי שעה. או מיראת הפסד ממון. כמו נפלה דליקה בעיר. יקום מיד במהרה. ולא יתרשל. או אם יצטרך ללכת לעבודת השר יקום ואל יתרשל. פן יעלילו עליו. או למצוא חן בעיני השר. על אחת כמה וכמה לעבודת מלך מלכי המלכים הקב"ה שיש לו להזהר לקום במהירות ובזריזות. ויירא פן יאחר לבא.


So then (he should not roll over¹⁶ upon his bed in the way of a sluggard),¹⁷ but rather with speed and vigor let him rise immediately.¹⁸ But if he be disfigured by plague and it should cause him to go about in a lazy way,¹⁹ let him reply His words²⁰ to him and pay attention²¹ to it, for if a creditor -- whether gentile or another man²² – shall call after him²³, he should rise immediately, whether because of the honor due to the man, whether because of the momentary gain that it shall bring to him,²⁴ or from fear of financial loss, like falling on the fuel of the fire, let him get up immediately, speedily, and not be lax. Or, if he needs to go to the service of the prince²⁵ let him get up and not be lax. Otherwise, they may heap abuse upon him.²⁶ Or [just] to find favor in the eyes of the prince.²⁷ How much the more so in the service of the King of Kings of Kings, the Holy One Blessed be He, so he must take heed to rise with speed, vigorousness, and awe, else he may be late to come.²⁸
III²⁹ And how good and pleasant it is if he should rise before the light of the morning to supplicate himself before his Creator and to direct the hours of the different night-watches dividing the night into three equal parts³⁰, since these are the times the Holy One³¹ is reminded of the destruction of the Temple and the exile of Israel among the nations and the prayer that a man may pray at that hour about the destruction and the exile is desired and his supplication falls before the Omnipresent One, whether he lengthens or shortens³² [his prayers] so long as he directs his heart (BT *Berachot* 5b) with his supplications, for better to say little with intention than much without intention.³³

footnotes:
¹⁶ Three editions spell with ס while one spells with ש. Meah She'arim points with ָkamatz; Moriah points with chirik; Vilna spells with ס. Thus the verb יָסוב / יִסוב would be parsed qal: jussive: 3ms: ס-ב-ב, do not turn about. The vocalization is a distinction without a difference. Zilber spells ישב: qal: jussive: 3ms: ש-ו-ב, do not return. Vilna: לא יסוב; others אל .
¹⁷ Square brackets and alternative reading: he should not [lie down on his bed in the manner of the sluggard,] in Vilna edition. N.b. the definite article in this variation.Makor ha-Yirah: : this is the version in the old editions. The מ"א wrote get up immediately not only so (דוקא) but rather stay (שהה) a little as is spelled out (?) in BT, Gittin 70. Therefore our rabbi wrote precisely (דוקא) that he should not sit in bed in a lazy manner but before he gets up from his bed it is good or well that he should tarry a little in his sleep. (אוהל יוסף) Now it seems to me that where our rabbi wrote to sit (יושב) its meaning is is like “to dwell in praise” (יושב תהילות) or “so you dwelt in Kadesh (many days)” (Deut. 1:46) which is the language of delay.
¹⁸ See e.g., Shulchan Aruch1:1. See n. 28.
¹⁹ Hebrew unclear.Makor ha-Yirah: BT Kiddushin 31a, Berachot 43b. SA (__) 2:6. “Our rabbi explains that as is said there the reason is as if the footsteps of the shekhinah were pressing, His glory fills the whole world. since the Shekhina is above his head, it is like as if he removes the fear from off of him, so writes our rabbi, as in # 105, and in another gloss Rabbenu Nissim [Gerondi(?)] on Kiddushin (31a) ‘in order that it appear that he has a lord over him and around him that sees and knows all his hiding places (מצפוניו).’
²⁰ That is, God’s words.
²¹ lit., “set his heart upon it.”
²² Vilna: בעל חוב כותי; Zilber ed.: בעל חוב גוי. Both mean "a gentile creditor;" כותי may be a nod to medieval Christian censors. Moriah and Meah She’arim read בעל חוב או אדם אחר, “a creditor or another man.” Negrin ed. גוי בעל חוב
²³ Negrin ed. deletes the word אליו
²⁴ Vilna: [שיביא] (שיבא), (shall come) [it shall bring to him]; Zilber ed: שיבא "shall come"; Moriah and Meah She’arim both have שיביא “that it shall bring”
²⁵ Zilber ed., Moriah, Mea She’arim all have המלך, "the king"; i.e., the gentile authorities.
²⁶ The verb עלל does not occur in the hiphil according to Jastrow, but in the kal it means “abuse.”
²⁷ Again, Zilber ed., Moriah, Mea She’arim read המלך, the king.
²⁸ Makor ha-Yirah: (at his n. 13) [He gives a list of halachic authorities for early rising:] M. Avot 4. (but quoting R. Yehuda b. Teimah, M. Avot 5:20); Shulchan Aruch, Orach Chaim 1:1, Tur 1:1, 2:1, R. Moses Isserles (mappah, gloss on Shulchan Aruch 1:1). And the ב"ח draws an inference that despite that he agrees about rising early, he should get up before the light of the morning.So it is in the older edition, and our rabbi writes here three which are really six reasons which by their hand a man should wake up to conquer his natural inclinations to get up immediately: (a) fear of a man and respect due the man, (b) financial gain and loss, (c) fear and respect of the king to find favor in his eyes. And not to connect solidifying (?) that to write of the most stubborn of them to learn, how much the more so to rise immediately because of the King of Kings, the Holy One Blessed be He. Since just as knowledge has no conclusion, feeling has no conclusion, and each one gets up according to his own marker and level just as one who is a prince and a head does not connect himself to the accounting of fear and honor of man … but this should be understood… thus as written in Duties of the Heart, 30 accountings but for each accounting a man has it in his ability to reverse the accounting from one extreme to the other and to bring himself close to the Blessed Creator. And on the other hand this one must explain always excite a man’s good impulse over his evil appetite, and there is no more room to continue here.
²⁹ Negrin ed. includes a whole section here labeled (ג) which does not appear in the other four eds. It is in square script, as part of the text, not the Negrin gloss.
³⁰ Lit., “which are one-third of the night and at the end [of that one] the second third of the night and to the end of the night…”
³¹ הב"ה = הברוך הוא
³² אחד המרבה ואחד הממעיט This phrase appears in the regular text at line 54 in the context of a discussion of prayer.
³³ AMEN (III). Negrin does not discuss the injunction to rise swiftly. However, he recommends that as soon as a man rises, he should learn the Torah portions of the Binding of Isaac (Gen. 22) and of the Manna (citing the Tur). Learning the Binding of Isaac, he explains will lessen God’s harsh judgment of us (in mystical thinking, by sympathetically linking us to the meritorious actions of our ancestors) and learning the portion of Manna will mystically invoke blessing and prosperity upon us, too. He mentions that it is not necessary to learn the Binding of Isaac on Shabbat because on that day God’s judgment is dormant and God feels only loving mercy (רחמים). Instead, one should learn the 10 commandments for the sake of mystically unifying the disparate aspects of God. For the same reasons, he recommends learning the portions of the Torah involving the daily sacrifices in the ancient Temple, as a way of symbolically offering them ourselves, and thereby mystically invoking God’s love.



## Paragraph 14

###### Sefer HaYirah 14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/14)


וכל מעשיו יחשוב לעשות לשם שמים. ולפי כחו ילך בדרכי החכמים. ויהיה נכנע.


Now, he should think to do all of his deeds for the sake of Heaven. And he should, as much as he is able, go by the way of the sages. But let him be humble.



## Paragraph 15

###### Sefer HaYirah 15

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/15](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/15)


אל יעמוד ערום ממטתו אף מיושב. כמו שהיה מתפאר ר' יוסי ז"ל מימי לא ראו קורות ביתי אמרי חלוקי. אך יקח חלוקו ויכניס בו ראשו וזרועיו ואז בקומו יהיה מכוסה. ואל יאמר הנני בחדרי חדרים בבית אפל מי רואני ומי (וידעני) [יודעני]. כי מלא כל הארץ כבודו. לפניו כחשכה כאורה.


Let him not get out of bed naked but only sit up. As Rabbi Yossi explained, “In all my life the inside of my tunic did not see the beams of my house.³⁴ Instead, let him take his undershirt and put his head and arms in it, so that when he gets up he will be covered. And he should not say, “Why, here I am in the inner rooms of a dark house, who will see me and who will know it is me?”³⁵ it is written, מי יועדני ומי רואני, “who will designate me and who will see me?” and it is written in the ב"ח (=בדפוס חדש?) new edition, but this language is to say, ‘who will know it is me, why, there is no man with me and even if there were a man with me, who would see me in the middle of the night?,’ as in the verse in Isaiah, “(…and their works are in the dark,) and they say ‘who sees us?’ and ‘who knows us?’” (Is. 29:15). Not like in *sefer ha-Yirah,*who will designate me, from language of witnessing. So far his words. And on the basis of this explanation it is possible to say what our Rabbi meant by [the apparent redundancy of] “inner rooms” and “dark house”: “will know me,” in the inner rooms and “wil see me” in the dark house. for the whole earth is full of His glory. Before Him, the darkness is like light.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁴ Makor ha-Yirah: BT, Shabbat 118b. Shulchan Aruch 2, Be’ur ha’Gra (Vilna Gaon). That is, he would not pull his undershirt off over his head, thereby turning it inside out and exposing it to the rafters above. We see the value of modesty here, an important value underlying this entire work. See also Leviticus Rabbah 20:11!
³⁵ Vilna: [יודעני] (וידעני): (and will know me) [will know me]. Zilber ed.: (יועדני)[יודעני] [will know me] (will designate me) (?). Moriah: יוֹעִדֵנִי, will designate me? Meah She'arim: מעדני, will designate me.Makor ha-Yirah: (with respect to the textual variance): In the Tur
³⁶ AMEN (ה): The reason for this is that the man’s limbs hint at things of the upper worlds, as Job wrote: (19:26) “…from my body, I would see God.” Even the [sign of] the holy covenant [i.e., the circumcision] and therefore it is not fitting to reveal them, for the honor of God is in the secret of his limbs.Negrin further comments on the issue of modesty (his ו’): the inner spark (הפנימי) is pure, but the outer side (הצד החיצון) is susceptible to impurity. Therefore it is not fitting to make the inner outer and the outer inner.



## Paragraph 16

###### Sefer HaYirah 16

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/16](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/16)


וילבש בגדיו. ויהיה לו טלית קטן וילבשנו מתחת למדיו. כי עיקר מצות ציצית לזכרון ללבשו תמיד. כי לזכרון המצוה נתנה. ולמען לא יסור האדם אחרי שרירות לבו ואחרי מראה עיניו המחטיאים את הגוף. והדרך הזה לא ימצא כי אם בלכתו בדרך אשר דברי ערוה מצויים שם.


Now he should put on his clothes. He should have a tallit katan³⁷ and let him put it on underneath his outer garments³⁸ for the crux of the commandment of wearing fringes is always remembering when getting dressed.³⁹ Because the commandment was given for remembering, and so that a man should not turn away after the stubbornness of his heart or after the gaze of his eyes which cause his body to sin. But this way will not be found unless he should go on the way in which the naked things ⁴⁰. One will not stray into sin unless he goes on a way where he will find temptations. are found.⁴¹

footnotes:
³⁷ That is, a four-cornered, fringed ritual undergarment meant for wearing all day.
³⁸ Negrin ed. תכף בקומו כדי שלא ילך ארבע אמות בלא ציצית, “when he gets up in so that he should not walk four cubits without ritual fringes...”
³⁹ Zilber ed. deletes the phrase כי עיקר מצוה and simply says כי לזכרון המצוה נתנה, “because the commandment was given for memory.”
⁴⁰ Lev. 18, Acharei Mot
⁴¹ AMEN (ח): Negrin quotes from Zohar, Shelach Lecha 83:331 -- apparently Tallit gadol and tallit katan are symbolic.

על כן ילבש אותו מיד בלא ברכה אם לא רחץ את ידיו. וטוב להיות זריז ליטול ידיו קודם ויברך עובר לעשייתו.


Therefore let him dress himself immediately without a blessing if he has not washed his hands. But it is good to be strict to wash his hands first⁴² and to bless after he has done it.⁴³

footnotes:
⁴² AMEN (ט): The explanation of “first” -- that he touch his clothes according to the custom of the first Chassidim [i.e., in Hasmonean times] (See note כ) who would prepare vessels of water by their bed in order to wash their hands and purify them and not handle their clothes with their impure hands, as mentioned in the Zohar, Bereisheet p. 36:146, and in such manner they would bless before doing it.AMEN (כ) Negrin continues at his XX: “The rabbi ends here, but I need to explain: Know that it is forbidden for a man to walk 4 cubits with impure hands. The first chassidim would prepare a ladle of water by their beds so that when they stirred in the night they washed their hands while they were still in bed in order that they not contaminate their clothes with impure hands.”
⁴³ עובר לעשייתוֹ – high flown Hebrew in original.

Now, let a man go and and let him take care of his needs⁴⁹ in the bathroom.⁵⁰

footnotes:
⁴⁹ lit, “check himself.”
⁵⁰ AMEN (XIV). Negrin sets forth a prayer that one entering the bathroom should recite, calling on the ministering angels who accompany each person to guard him and help him: “be clean, O honorable holy ministering angels of the most high, guard me, guard me, help me, help me, wait upon me as I enter until I leave, for such is the way of men.” (Tur, top of § 3). He then discusses the need to repeat “guard me,” and “help me.”



## Paragraph 24

###### Sefer HaYirah 24

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/24](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/24)


ושם יהיה צנוע. אל יגלה עצמו כי אם לפניו טפח. ומאחריו טפחיים. והאשה מלאחריה טפח. ולפניה ולא כלום.


And there he should be modest.⁵¹ He should not expose himself but for a hands-breadth in front of him and two hands-breadths behind him. And for a woman, one hands-breadth behind her and in front of her, nothing.⁵² and the *Tur, Orach Chaim*, and the scrivener’s error has falllen into the books, in front instead of behind and behind instead of in front. Now the words of our rabbi do not mention but in the new printing (?) הב"ח writes that since even in Sefer ha-Yirah the version is so, it is difficult to dismiss as a scrivener’s error, one falling in all the books but rather it is certain that the versions in the *shas* ibid, and distinguish between a man and a woman, it appears that as to the woman the stricture is greater. But in the new printing (?) it explicates a different reason: to choose the stricter of the two versions. And see in the *Mishnah Berurah* in the name of the Latter [sages]. (אחרונים)

footnotes:
⁵¹ Negrin ed., “Now let him be modest in the bathroom and not expose himself until he sits; afterwards he may expose in front of him two hands-breadths and behind him one hands-breadth; and a woman, behind her one handsbreadth and and in front of her, nothing.” (emphasis mine.)
⁵² AMEN (XV). From here, one must be strict not to expose even his arm when he lays tefillin because all the limbs of a person hint at great and awesome things and there is not praise like modesty.Once again, Negrin shows the kabbalistic idea that the limbs of a person reflect aspects of the divine being.Negrin continues: When he sits, he should face south with his back to the north. The south is associated with [the sefirah of] Hesed, the characteristic of Abraham, as is said, “loyal love (חסד) to Abraham” (Micah 7:20 following Koren), and his back to the north, as is said, “out of the north the evil shall break forth,” (Jer. 1:14, Koren). And he should not sit between east to west, since the Shekhina is in the west and since everlasting memory לזכר בעלמא … is between east to west it is forbidden in whatever place may be. And by my opinion the secret [meaning] of east to west the crown of Tiferet, in order not to divide them, and it is enough to understand.Makor ha-Yirah: But see, in the Shas (the Talmud) BT Berachot 62b, 23__ it is explained the opposite, (as we see in the Negrin ed.), in front of him two hands-breadths because of spouting urine. And behind him one hands-breadth, and so it turns out that there is no distinction between a man and a woman as to the matter of behind. But according to the edition of our rabbi goes the Bet-Yosef



## Paragraph 25

###### Sefer HaYirah 25

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/25](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/25)


ולא ישתין מעומד פן יהיו ניצוצות ניתזות על רגליו ויראה ככרות שפכה. אלא יעמוד במקום גבוה או [ישתין] בעפר תיחוח. ואם ישתין מעומד יזהר פן יאחוז באמה אלא מעטרה ולמטה לצד הארץ ואף אם הוא נשוי. כי אחרי אשר העונש גדול כל כך שהוא מביא מבול לעולם מאד יש לו לאדם להתחזק ולקדש עצמו אף במותר לו. וכן רבינו הקדוש מימיו לא הכניס ידיו למטה מאבנטו.


He should not urinate standing up in case there be sprinkles splashing upon his legs and he should appear like one whose urinary canal is mutilated. Rather, let him stand in a high place or urinate in dust. But if he does urinate standing up he should take care lest he take hold of the membrum virile – rather, [he should hold it] from the corona on down the lower side⁵³ even if he be married.⁵⁴ 13a; Shulchan Aruch __ 3:13,14. “Even if he be married…” see the *Beit Yosef* (ב"י) which brings our Rabbi’s words (as) the source for the *Tur*, which forbids (it (masturbation)) even for a married man, and where the *Beit Yosef* explains the characteristic of piety he means according to our Rabbi, and thus it is decided in the Shulchan Aruch. [He appears to disagree with the *Taz* (Turei Zahav?)] *Rambam*, ch. 21 of Laws of forbidden sexual entry: it is not permitted to a married man except to urinate; *Be’ur ha’Gra* (Vilna Gaon); Shabbat 41a (R. Abahu was married.) For the punishment for it⁵⁵ is so great as to destroy the world.⁵⁶ He must strengthen himself and sanctify himself, even in what is permitted to him. And thus our holy Rabbi: in all his days he did not put his hand under his belt.⁵⁷

footnotes:
⁵³ lit., “to the side of the earth.”
⁵⁴ Evidently, a man should never masturbate, nor even touch the top of his own penis.Makor ha-Yirah: BT Niddah
⁵⁵ That is, masturbation.
⁵⁶ lit., “bring the flood to the world.” Presumably capital punishment, or he may refer to a divine punishment.
⁵⁷ BT, Shabbat 118b.

XX.
He should wash his hands completely.⁶² with water if he has it. He should wash them exceedingly well and recite the *al netilat yadayim*, “concerning the washing of the hands” blessing over them.⁶³

footnotes:
⁶² Lit., "with cleanness."
⁶³ AMEN: (A continuation of the note above) Now know that when a man sleeps at night the soul goes out from his body and immediately שורה the unclean spirit upon his hands, and the reason [for] the hands is that they are against two arms of teh world/eternal which seize powers of impurity and therefore the שורה upon the hands. Now the one who walks four cubits with impure hands is liable for death from Heaven since he brought to the Other in his hands and about him it is said, “The tents of robbers prosper, and they who provoke God are secure; they who bring their God in their hand.” (Job 12:6 following Koren). Who they who provoke God? They who bring their Other in their hand. etc.In other words, the hands are vectors of dangerous ritual impurity, and again we see Negrin’s comparison of the human body to the cosmic map.

Then afterwards he should lay the arm-tefillin⁶⁹ on the left arm above the part that they call the elbow in the common tongue,⁷⁰, *kovri,* or *kovdo* in Spanish or French, and there seems to be much confusion among the editions. "Elbow" is my emendation. adjusted slightly toward the heart and recite the blessing, “…to lay tefillin.” After that he should put on the head-tefillin next to the brain⁷¹ and recite the “…about the commandment of tefillin” blessing.

וצריך לברך כשימשמש זמן גדול אחר הנחתן. דקיימא לן כרבנן דבי רב אשרי דכל אימת דממשמשי מברכי.


And he needs to bless when he handles them for a long time after putting them on, as it is established⁷⁵ according to the Rabbis in the name of R. Ashi: “every time I handled (them) I blessed (them).”⁷⁶, 46a.

footnotes:
⁷⁵ Vilna, Zilber ed., דקיימא לן, Moriah, Me’ah She’arim דְּקַיְמָא לָן.
⁷⁶ BT, Sukkah



## Paragraph 43

###### Sefer HaYirah 43

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/43](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/43)


ומפי מורי הרב ר' שמואל בר' שניאור שמעתי דאין צריך לברך משום משמוש אלא כשהן נעקרין ממקומן והוא מחזירן למקומן.


But according to⁷⁷ my teacher R. Shmuel bar Shneur⁷⁸ I heard that one does not need to bless on account of handling except when they are taken out from their place and he returns them to their place.

Now, with regard to the matter of sleep there are three parts to the matter: When they are on his head he may nap but not sleep.⁷⁹

footnotes:
⁷⁹ Lit., occasional sleep but not fixed.



## Paragraph 45

But when they are held in his hand he may not sleep or nap in case they fall from his hand.

Now if he puts them on where he lies down let him spread a blanket over them and sleep. And in them he may sleep [something] between a nap and a full sleep.⁸⁰ 26; *Berachot* 20. SA __ 44. Also the opinion of the *Rosh* is so.

When he goes to the bathroom he takes them off outside the four cubits [of the bathroom] or winds them up and sets them down in a vessel inside a vessel in his pocket next to his heart.



## Paragraph 48

###### Sefer HaYirah 48

**Then he should go** to the synagogue. And when he comes close to the synagogue he should quicken his steps, as is written, “In the house of God let us walk in excitement." (Ps. 55:15)⁸¹

footnotes:
⁸¹ Translation mine.



## Paragraph 49

###### Sefer HaYirah 49

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/49](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/49)

But he should check his shoes in case they are filthy or soiled as [King] Solomon said in his Wisdom: “Guard your foot when you go to the house of God.” (Ecc., 4:17)



## Paragraph 51

###### Sefer HaYirah 51

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/51](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/51)


ויבא לבית הכנסת. ואומר בכניסתו ואני ברוב חסדך אבא ביתך אשתחוה אל היכל קדשך ביראתך. וישב במקומו.


He should come to the synagogue and say when he enters, “As for me, in your great love I shall come to your house, I shall bow down to your holy temple in fear of You,” and he should sit in his seat. (Ps. 5:8) ⁸²

⁸² In other words, he should recite מה טובו as is reflected in the prayerbook.



## Paragraph 52

###### Sefer HaYirah 52

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/52](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/52)

If he has another tallis he should cover himself in it and recite the blessing, “…to wrap oneself in *tzitzit*.”⁸³

footnotes:
⁸³ Again, evidence that the wealth of the community may have been quite modest. Tallitot are not that expensive, and can be made by hand if need be.



## Paragraph 53

###### Sefer HaYirah 53

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/53](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/53)


וישב ולא יפתח פיו עד שישהה מעט. וישיב אל לבו לפני מי הוא עומד. ומי השומע דבריו. ואז ילבש חרדה ואימה וזיע ורתת.


Then he should sit and not open his mouth until he pauses a little and answers in his heart: Before Whom he stands and Who is the One hearing his words. And thus he will be seized by⁸⁴ trembling, fear, sweating, and feeling awestruck.

footnotes:
⁸⁴ ועז ילבש. Lit., he will wear trembling... The pointed eds. put this in the qal, but in the rabbinic binyan afeyl אַלְבֵּיש is to seize. I would therefore point this as יַלְבֵּש .



## Paragraph 54

###### Sefer HaYirah 54

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/54](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/54)


ויפתח תפלתו על הסדר אחד המרבה ואחד הממעיט. ובלבד שיכוין לבו לשמים.


Now let him begin his prayer in the [proper] order, [it does not matter whether] one that lengthens or one that shortens,⁸⁵ only that [so long as] he directs his heart to heaven.⁸⁶ 5b; *Menachot* 110a. Only Mea She'arim gives the second citation.

footnotes:
⁸⁵ Referring to two styles of praying: speaking slowly versus speaking rapidly, but the same keva.

He should prepare his clothes, as is said, (Amos 4:12) “Prepare to meet your God, Israel.”



## Paragraph 56

###### Sefer HaYirah 56

## Paragraph 57

אך ישמיע לאזניו מה שיוציא מפיו.


But he should speak only loud enough so that his own ears hear what comes out of his mouth.



## Paragraph 58

###### Sefer HaYirah 58

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/58](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/58)


ויחתוך בשפתיו כל תיבה ותיבה. ואל יבלעם. גם סוף התיבות יחתוך להפרידם מתיבה הסמוכה לה פן יכשל בספרו בגוים את כבודו. שבחי ירושלים את. וכן כל את ואותו ואותה הסמוכים למ"ם צריך להפרידם זה מזה. וכן אלהיכם אמת. וכיוצא באלה.


XXX.
He should clearly enunciate⁸⁸ each and every letter. He should not swallow them.⁸⁹ (XXI) Also he should enunciate the end of the words to separate them from the word next to it in case he stumbles over (Ps. 96:3) “tell of His glory among the gentiles…”⁹⁰ (Ps. 147:12) “… praises of Jersualem…”⁹¹ And so it is with each and every “ et, ve'otoh, ve’otah.”⁹² that is next to a [letter] mem:⁹³ [one] needs to separate them, this one from that one, and so with “ [adonai] elocheichem emet” and [such] as come out with these.⁹⁴

footnotes:
⁸⁸ Lit., "cut with his lips."
⁸⁹ Vilna and Mea She'arim: ואל יבלען . Zilber ed. and Moriah: ואל יבלעם
⁹⁰ Moriah helpfully inserts a makkef: ב-סַפְרוּ בַגוּיִם . Ps. 96:3.
⁹¹ Ps. 147:12.
⁹² untranslatable Hebrew direct object markers, ּאֶת, וְאוֹתוֹ, וְאוֹתָהּ
⁹³ AMEN (XXX): Negrin comments at great length on the importance of proper pronunciation. First, he says that the words and letters must be carefully separated because each and every word and letter in the Torah is a hint of the sefirot and the upper worlds. One should sing “Baruch she’amar” beautifully because it contains 87 (פ"ז) letters, which equals “his head is most fine gold” ( ראשו כתם פז) (Song of Songs, 5:11). Such is Ashkenazi practice, established by the men of the Great Assembly (c.f. M. Avot 1:1) But it is important not to add to the 87 letters because it would detract from the kavvanah. They also say it while standing in all lands of Ashkenaz and Tzarfat. “Baruch she’amar” contains 10 “baruch…” statements, according to the 10 statements by which the world was created (i.e., “And God said, ‘let there be light,’ and there was light...”) He continues through the early parts of the morning prayer service, mentioning “kol ha’neshamah t’hallel yah” and “yishtabach” mentioning the Talmudic requirement to recite praise with song and melody before praying (the Amidah) [BT Berachot 32a]. He adds that the air is full of evil spirits and that prayer will protect against them. Later in this lengthy comment he says that when reciting פותֵחַ את ידיך in the Ashrei, one should pronounce ידיך as יוּדיך (“your yuds”) as the secret meaning of the two letterֿs yud (perhaps in the abbreviation of the divine name יְיָ) that draw down the great light and the holy flow into the world. This is also the meaning of the 3 letters yud in the [beginning of each phrase of the] priestly blessing. And from Ashrei through “l’olam va’ed” there are 613 letters, so that by reciting them correctly it is as if one had fulfilled all 613 mitzvot. The ten psalms that begin and end with “halleluyah” are compared to praise of a king. But if one cannot recite them all, he should say at least: ברוך שאמר, תהילה לדוד, הללו אל בקדשו which accompish something of the same purpose. Reciting shirat ha-yam will merit seeing the messiah, as when Israel first recited it they were crowned with the same crown that God will in the future crown the messiah. It has 18 verses corresponding to the 18 vertebrae in the spine [a concept seen elsewhere in Sefer ha-Yirah] adn the placement of “then Israel saw the Egyptians dead upon the shore of the sea” next to “so Moses sang” hinting at the death of the nations but the life of Israel. He discusses the need to pronounce the letter Kaf in מי כָּמוֹךָ to avoid language of blasphemy or else it would sound like “Michah is like you, O God” asserting that the Biblical prophet Micah secreted away the pronunciation of the divine Name. Negrin segues into how responding forcefully enough to the prayers such as “amen” or “y’hei shmei raba” or “baruch hashem hamevorach” causes unfavorable heavenly judgments to be ripped up. He goes so far as to say that the letters of the prayers should be said with as much exactitude as the letters of the Torah. Eventually, he concludes by saying each and every letter is like a candle corresponding to one of the 248 positive mitzvot (the metaphor comes from “the commandment is a lamp and Torah is light.” Prov. 6:23)
⁹⁴ There are siddurim that actually place stars over such letters to make clear the need to enunciate where the letters would run into one another.



## Paragraph 59

###### Sefer HaYirah 59

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/59](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/59)


ואל יהיה מאריך בתפלתו יותר מדאי בצבור פן יחשבוהו (לכבוד) [ליהיר] ולץ:


He should not be one who lengthens his prayer too much in public in case they should think him a a show-off or a scoffer.⁹⁵

footnotes:
⁹⁵ Vilna: [ליהיר] (לכבד). Zilber ed. לכבד ולץ. Moriah: לְכָבֵד וְלֵץ. Mea She'arim: לְיָהִיר ולֵץ. Translation follows Mea She'arim here.



## Paragraph 60

###### Sefer HaYirah 60

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/60](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/60)


ובקריאת שמע יהיה נזהר שיוציא בשפתיו וישמיע לאזניו רמ"ח תיבות שבו.


**When he recites**⁹⁶ the Sh'ma he should be very careful to utter with his lips and hear with his ears all 248 letters that are in it.

footnotes:
⁹⁶ Boldface in Vilna and Moriah.



## Paragraph 61

###### Sefer HaYirah 61

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/61](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/61)


ויתן ריוח בין הדבקים כדברי רבותינו ע"ה בכל לבבך ועל לבבך וכיוצא בהן.


But he must allow an interval between words that may easily run into one another (BT, Berachot 15b). According to the words of our Rabbis, peace be upon them, “with all of your heart” and “upon your heart” and [such] as come out with them.



## Paragraph 62

###### Sefer HaYirah 62

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/62](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/62)


ובכל אחד ואחד משלשה שמות האמורים בפסוק הראשון יאריך עד שיחשוב היה הוה ויהיה. ולא יאריך באלף דאחד.


And with each and every one of the three [divine] names that are in the first line⁹⁷ he should lengthen them until he thinks [of] past, present, and future but he should not lengthen the aleph ("one").

footnotes:
⁹⁷ The first line of the Sh'ma has three occurrences of the divine name: יי ,אלוקינו and יי the second time.



## Paragraph 63

###### Sefer HaYirah 63

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/63](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/63)


ולא ימהר בחית לקרותה בחטף.


And he should not hurry the [letter] chet [in the word echad] as if to read it with a chataf.⁹⁸

footnotes:
⁹⁸ That is, one should not shorten the ח' in the word אחד to read אֶחָד as if it were אֶחֳד.



## Paragraph 64

###### Sefer HaYirah 64

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/64](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/64)


ויאריך בדלת עד שיחשוב בלבבו שבורא עולם הוא מלך למעלה ולמטה בשמים ובארץ וארבע רוחות העולם ממזרח וממערב צפון ודרום ותהום רבה ורמ"ח איברים שבו.


And he should lengthen the [letter] dalet [in the word echad] until he considers in his heart that the Creator of the world is king above and below, in heaven and on earth, and the four winds of the world, from east and the west, north and south, and the great depth, and the 248 limbs that are in him.



## Paragraph 65

###### Sefer HaYirah 65

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/65](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/65)


ואם לא יוכל לכוין כל כך יכוין ה' שהוא עתה אלהינו עתיד להיות אחד.


But if he just cannot direct his heart so much (have so much kavvanah) let him direct himself to Hashem, who is now our God, in the future to be one.⁹⁹, “on that day the age-old dream shall be fulfilled: He shall be one and His name shall be one.” This is a very interesting law, for Rabbenu Yonah, who seems so dour and so exacting, to be so understanding of and lenient here toward human nature. Also very interesting temporal theology.

footnotes:
⁹⁹ C.f. Aleynu lishabeach



## Paragraph 66

###### Sefer HaYirah 66

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/66](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/66)


וכל פעם בהזכירו שם המיוחד יחשוב בו פירוש קריאתו שהוא אדון כל.


And each time he mentions the Specific Name¹⁰⁰ let him think on it the meaning of its reading: that He is Lord of all.

footnotes:
¹⁰⁰ That is, the four-letter name of God.



## Paragraph 67

###### Sefer HaYirah 67

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/67](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/67)


בכל לבבך. בשני יצריך. יצר טוב ויצר רע. ובכל נפשך. אפי' נוטל נשמתך. ובכל מאדך. בכל מדה ומדה שהוא מודד לך.


[When he recites] “With all your heart,” he will need [to meditate] on two [things]:¹⁰¹ the good appetite and the evil appetite. “And with all your soul,” [is] even taking your soul.¹⁰² “And with all your might,” [means] Whatever measure that He measured out for you (M. Berachot, 9:5. BT, Berachot 54a).¹⁰³

footnotes:
¹⁰¹ בשני יצרך
¹⁰² i.e., even martyrdom for the sanctification of the Divine Name. Such practice was a very real possibility in medieval Christian Europe.
¹⁰³ Translation following Prof. M. Jastrow.

###### Sefer HaYirah 68

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/68](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/68)

[At] “Which I command you today,” let him pause and consider, “ today they were commanded to me."



## Paragraph 69

###### Sefer HaYirah 69

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/69](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/69)

“Upon your heart,” thus far, the intention of the heart and the crux of the acceptance of the Yoke of the kingdom of heaven, and even when walking on the road, being directed until he arrives at “upon your heart.”



## Paragraph 70

###### Sefer HaYirah 70

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/70](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/70)

## Paragraph 71

###### Sefer HaYirah 71

## Paragraph 72

###### Sefer HaYirah 72

Plus, he should enunciate [the letter] zayin in [the word] Tizkaru ("you shall remember");¹⁰⁵

footnotes:
¹⁰⁵ לְמַעַן תִּזְכְּרוּ וַעֲשִׂיתֶם | אֶת-כָּל-מִצְוֹתָי, at the conclusion of the recitation of the Sh'ma.



## Paragraph 75

ואל ירמוז בעיניו. ויקרוץ בשפתיו. וימלול ברגליו ויורה באצבעותיו. כי תועבת ה' כל עושה אלה.


**and he should not**¹⁰⁶ wink¹⁰⁷ 62b for use of רמז to mean "wink." nor gesticulate with his lips¹⁰⁸ 19b. or one who rubs his legs¹⁰⁹ or one who points with his fingers. For anyone who does these is an abomination of the LORD.¹¹⁰

¹⁰⁶ Boldface in Moriah, Mea She'arim.
¹⁰⁷ See BT Shabbat

¹¹⁰ See e.g. Prov. 6:12.



## Paragraph 76

###### Sefer HaYirah 76

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/76](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/76)


ובתפלתו מיושב אל יסמוך לאחריו. ולא יהא מוטה לצדדין דרך גאוה. אלא ישב וראשו כפוף. שלא יראה פני היושב נגדו חוץ לארבע אמותיו. ושני אציליו על עצם הירך מקום חיבורו לגוף מכאן ומכאן.


Now, one who sits in prayer should not lean on [something] behind him, nor shall he lean to his sides in the manner of a proud person. Rather, he should sit with his head bowed so that he should not see the face of the person sitting in front of him beyond his four cubits. And his arms aligned with the body, with hands crossed over the waist.



## Paragraph 77

And his two hands [should be] under his clothes, the right over the left in front of his navel and he should not stretch his legs but rather sit upright upon his "sit” bones in order that he should be more trembling and fearful.

He should bow quickly at “you are blessed” and he should straighten up slowly at the divine] Name;¹¹³ he should bow at the beginning and the end of the modim (thanksgiving section of the Amidah) and no more in the eighteen [benedictions of the Amidah].

But¹¹⁴ when he takes three steps he gives peace beginning with the left, to the right and bows, before him and bows.¹¹⁵ after saying יהיו לרצון אמרי פי...

footnotes:
¹¹⁴ אבל. A stronger "but" than the usual prefixed ו.
¹¹⁵ i.e., when finishing the Amidah



## Paragraph 85

###### Sefer HaYirah 85

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/85](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/85)


ותהיה הכריעה כמו שאמרו חכמים עד שיתפקקו כל החליות שבשדרה. ועד שיראה איסר כנגד לבו. וזהו בשר ברוחב איסר בין פרק חליות השדרה כנגד הלב. וכשאדם זקוף מובלע ואינו נראה. וכשהוא שוחה אז יתפרדו החוליות ונראה אותו בשר.


The bow should be as the sages said, until all the vertebrae in the spine¹¹⁶ shall protrude¹¹⁷ until he is able to see an *isar*¹¹⁸ of flesh opposite his heart -- now this is flesh the width of an *isar* between the part of the spine opposite the heart. And when the person straightens, [it] disappears and is not seen.¹¹⁹ And when he bows, the vertebrae of the spine separate themselves and the same flesh reappears.

footnotes:
¹¹⁶ חוליות שבסדרה
¹¹⁷ Zilber ed. יתפרקו, which could also mean "be loose," "be moved," "be bent," or "be flexed." Others יתפקקו. "are loosened." Translation follows explanation given in Schottenstein edition of the Talmud.
¹¹⁸ A roman coin.
¹¹⁹ BT, Berachot, 28b.

ואל יחזור מיד למקומו פן יחשב ככלב שב על קיאו.

¹²⁰ קיאו.



## Paragraph 87

###### Sefer HaYirah 87

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/87](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/87)


ולא ירוק בתפלתו. ואם נזדמן לו רוק מבליעו בכסותו.


He should not spit during his prayer.¹²¹, in the traditional line which has been edited out of most North American siddurim, even traditional one, but which appears in the ArtScroll siddur and in most Israeli siddurim: (שֶׁהֵם מִשְׁתַּחֲוִים לְהֶבֶל וָרִיק וּמִתְפַּלְלִים אֶל אֵל לֹא יוֹשִׁיעַ). I have seen a Lubavitch edition of the siddur which actually gives the stage direction to spit. Apparently, this custom is older than the Chassidic movement. See *BT, Sanh. 101a; JT Ber. III, 6d*. But if he is spat upon, he hides the spittle in his cloak.¹²². Translation following Jastrow.

footnotes:
¹²¹ As opposed to the chassidic practice of spitting during the Alenu
¹²² See BT, Ber. 24b; JT Sabb. VII, 10b

He should not even belch¹²³ or yawn,¹²⁴ but if he does yawn he puts his hand upon his chin or over his mouth.¹²⁵

¹²⁴ all eds. ולא יפהק (בלע"ז בדליי"ר). (unpointed eds. בלעז without abbreviation indicators). Which foreign language unclear.
¹²⁵ See BT, Ber. 24a.

But he should not make it fixed like the person who says, "it is a fixed law upon me every day, the obligation to say such and such prayers¹²⁸ lies upon me," but rather let him make [them] supplications before the Omnipresent One.¹²⁹

## Paragraph 93

###### Sefer HaYirah 93

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/93](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/93)


ואל יעיין בתפלתו לאמר כמה התפללתי בכוונה ראויה היא להתקבל. כי הוא משלשה דברים המזכירים עונותיו של אדם. כי מקטרגים עליו ואומרים וכי ראוי הוא לכך שתתקבל תפלתו והלא עשה כך וכך. אך יחשוב בלבבו כי מאד היה לו לעמוד בתפלה יותר. אולי יכפר אחד מעונותיו הקטנים. ויזכור כי בשר הוא רוח הולך ולא ישוב.


Let him not contemplate his prayer, saying, "I have prayed so much with intentionality, it is worthy to be accepted, for it is one of three things mentioned [in connection with] the sins of a person¹³⁰ for they¹³¹ accuse him about them¹³², saying, "Is it worthy that such a prayer should be accepted, and has he not done such and such?" But let him think in his heart that he really should have stood much longer in prayer, perhaps one of his small[er] sins would be forgiven, and let him remember that is he is [but] flesh [and blood] breath leaving and not returning.

footnotes:
¹³⁰ The others being repentance and charity (ותפילה, וצדקה, ותשובה מעבירין את רוע הגזרה). See High Holy Day liturgy.
¹³¹ That is, accusers on high, prosecuting angels.
¹³² Vilna, Zilber ed. עליהם "about them," Moriah, Me'ah She'arim עליו, "about it."

ומה יתרון לו להתגאות במעשיו הלא לכך נוצר. ואם על כרחו יגאה לבו. ישים אל לבו גאוני עולם שגועו ועברו מן העולם. אשר עשו טובה אלף ידות יותר ממנו.


And what gain is there for him to boast about his deeds? Should he not be on guard for such [as this]? But if his heart must grow proud, let him think about¹³³ great people of the world that fell away and passed away from the world but which did a thousand times more good than he.

¹³³ lit., "set in his heart."

## Paragraph 96

ואם ישבו אצלו בני אדם המדברים (ברצון) יניע שפתיו וידמה עצמו כמתפלל. ואל יתפאר לומר איני מדבר. כי כל המתפאר כמעט הוא נוטל את שכרו.


But if some people sat by him talking intentionally¹³⁶ let him shake his lips in disdain¹³⁷ and make himself quiet as when he prays. But he let him not lord over [them], saying, "it is not I who is talking," for every time one is haughty he takes away a little bit of his reward.¹³⁸

Now let him respond "amen" to each and every blessing¹³⁹ that is, phrase beginning (and possibly ending) with יה-וה ... וברוך אתה . For the one who responds "amen" is greater than the one who says the blessing and the gates of paradise open for him.

לעולם בין בשעת תפלה בין בשעת ברכה המזון כל שעה שאדם עסוק לדבר בשכינה לא יגע בבשרו.


**As long as**¹⁴³ a man is busy speaking of the *Shekhinah*¹⁴⁴ between the time of prayer and the time of the grace after meals, he shall not be plagued.¹⁴⁵

## Paragraph 101

###### Sefer HaYirah 101

Except in his hands¹⁴⁶ up to the forearm,¹⁴⁷ or in his neck as far as the shirt, but not in his legs, and not in all the rest of his flesh or his feet. But if he is plagued let him wash his hands or rub them¹⁴⁸ with dust [or with pebbles]¹⁴⁹ or with [wood] chips.¹⁵⁰ 59a

## Paragraph 102

###### Sefer HaYirah 102

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/102](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/102)

But if he needs to scratch, let him scratch over his undershirt; and he must not touch neither a cavity nor the hair of the arm-pit nor the area of the genitals.¹⁵¹ But if he does touch [them] he should wash his hands or rub them with dust, pebbles, or wood-chips.¹⁵²

¹⁵¹ ובית הערוה

## Paragraph 103

###### Sefer HaYirah 103

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/103](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/103)


ובשעת תפלה טפח מגולה אפילו באשתו ערוה. ואסור לקרות שמע ולהתפלל ולהסתכל בה.


Now if at the time of prayer, a hands-breadth is exposed -- even to his wife¹⁵³ -- of genital area, then it is forbidden to recite *shema* or to pray or look upon it.¹⁵⁴

footnotes:
¹⁵³ אפילו באשתו Translation of this halachah follows the interesting word order of the original.
¹⁵⁴ or "upon her."



## Paragraph 104

###### Sefer HaYirah 104

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/104](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/104)


ויצא מבית הכנסת בנחת בדרך כריעה ויאמר ה' נחני בצדקתך וגו' (תהלים ה) וילך.


**Then he goes**¹⁵⁵ out from the synagogue humbly and in a bowing manner;¹⁵⁶ he says, "Lead me, O LORD, in thy righteousness..." (Ps. 5:9), then he goes.¹⁵⁷

footnotes:
¹⁵⁵ Boldface in Moriah, Me'ah She'arim.
¹⁵⁶ בנחת ובדרך כריעה
¹⁵⁷ Ps. 5 does not appear to be a fixed part of the standard liturgy of today.



## Paragraph 105

###### Sefer HaYirah 105

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/105](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/105)


ובלכתו בדרך אל ישוח יותר מדאי. ואל ישפיל סודרו על עיניו פן יחשב ללעג.


But when he goes on his way,¹⁵⁸ he should not bend too much.¹⁵⁹ And he should not lower his scarf over his eyes lest he be thought a mockery.

footnotes:
¹⁵⁸ ובלכתו בדרך -- note the beautiful echo of ובלכתך בדרך from the ואהבת prayer.
¹⁵⁹ But see halachah #8, a man should always go about bent over.



## Paragraph 106

###### Sefer HaYirah 106

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/106](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/106)


ואל ילך בקומה זקופה. כי ההלוך בקומה זקופה דומה כמסלק המורא מעליו. אלא ילך במדה בינונית לא זקוף ולא כפוף יותר מדאי. אלא שלא יראה הבא כנגדו בפניו.


Nor should he walk with proud carriage¹⁶⁰ for one who walks at his full stature is like one who suspends the fear [of heaven] from upon him. But rather he should walk in an average manner, not erect but not too bent. Rather¹⁶¹ so that he should not look one who comes in front of him in the face.

footnotes:
¹⁶⁰ בקומה זקופה; at his fully erect stature.
¹⁶¹ Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: [עַד] (אֶלָּא). "(rather) [until]"



## Paragraph 107

###### Sefer HaYirah 107

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/107](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/107)


וישפיל עיניו שלא יראה חבירו כנגדו כמלא עיניו.


Let him lower his eyes so that he should not look his fellow right in the eye.



## Paragraph 108

###### Sefer HaYirah 108

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/108](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/108)


ואם פגע דבר ערוה כגון אשה בין יהודית בין נכרית בין נשואה בין פנויה. בין גדולה בין קטנה. יעצים עיניו. או יסובב לצד אחר שלא יראנה וכן אמר החכם אין לך דבר חוצץ בפני התאוה כעצימת העין.


And if he should come in contact with a thing of nakedness such as a woman, whether Jewish or gentile¹⁶², whether married or single, whether big or small, he should close his eyes or turn his face¹⁶³ aside so that he shall not see her. And so said the sage, "You have no such barrier¹⁶⁴ faced with of sexual desire as the closing of the eye."

footnotes:
¹⁶² ארמית lit., "Aramean." R. Yonah could not directly say "Christian" due to censorship.
¹⁶³ פניו, "his face," appears in Vilna, Zilber ed.; Moriah and Me'ah She'arim omit it.
¹⁶⁴ אין לך דבר חוצץ בפני התאוה. More literally, "you have no way of separation in the face of desire..."



## Paragraph 109

###### Sefer HaYirah 109

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/109](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/109)


וכן אם פגע חזיר וחזירה או כל בהמה נזקקין זה לזה אל יסתכל בהן.


And the same if he should come in contact with and boar and a sow or a dog and a bitch¹⁶⁵ or any animal in intercourse¹⁶⁶ with each other¹⁶⁷ -- he must not look upon them.

footnotes:
¹⁶⁵ Moriah, Me'ah She'arim omit או כלב ובלבתא
¹⁶⁶ Vilna, Zilber ed.: נזקקין (Rabbinic plural); Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: נִזְקָקִים (Biblical plural).
¹⁶⁷ Vilna, Zilber ed.: זה לזה; Moriah: זֶה לָזֶה (masc.) ; Me'ah She'arim: זוֹ לָזוֹ (fem.)

ויהיה מקדים בשלום כל אדם אפי' אינו יהודי כדי שיהיה אהוב ונחמד לבריות


And he should be the first to greet every man,¹⁶⁸ even a gentile¹⁶⁹ so that he should be beloved and precious to [God's] creatures.

footnotes:
¹⁶⁸ אדם, which this work has generally rendered, "person," but in this case "man" seems more appropriate given the following halachah.
¹⁶⁹ Vilna, Zilber ed.: כותי, lit. a "Cuthite," a Samaritan, but in eds. published under censorial influence, כותי frequently is a euphemism for גוי. Me'ah She'arim: אֵינוֹ-יִשרָאֵל, a non-Jew. Moriah: גוי, a gentile.

But he should not greet a woman, so that he may return to his home and family in peace.¹⁷⁰

footnotes:
¹⁷⁰ לביתו, in all eds., could also mean "to his wife."



## Paragraph 112

###### Sefer HaYirah 112

footnotes:
¹⁷¹ See halachot 31-32. If a man is able to have more than one pair of pants, he should keep a good pair for shul -- his "Sunday best," as it were.



## Paragraph 114

###### Sefer HaYirah 114

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/114](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/114)

And it would be good for him to learn [some Torah] immediately, since it is written, "they go from strength to strength," (Ps. 84:8),¹⁷² immediately, before he goes out to his business.

footnotes:
¹⁷² i.e., going from the strengthening activity of prayer to the strengthening activity of Torah study.

**But if**¹⁷³ he be a weak man and accustomed to breakfast, let him eat immediately.

footnotes:
¹⁷³ Boldface in Moriah, Me'ah She'arim.



## Paragraph 116

But he should wash his hands¹⁷⁴ first with a lot of water. At the very least he should wash with a quarter-log¹⁷⁵ 99a. of water.¹⁷⁶

¹⁷⁵ A Talmudic unit of liquid measurement roughly equal to one large glass. See BT Pesachim
¹⁷⁶ Vilna: [מים] (ממי) "(of water) [water]." Zilber ed. ממי. Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: מַיִם.

Let him pour water over his hands three times.¹⁷⁷

¹⁷⁷ Zilber cites sources explaining the purpose of each pouring.

And let him rub them¹⁷⁸ well, so that there should be no barrier¹⁷⁹ for any barrier to immersion [in a mikveh] is a barrier to [ritual] handwashing.

¹⁷⁸ Moriah: וִישַפְשְפֵם , others: וישפשפן. Is he talking about ritual handwashing or handwashing for cleanliness? I'm not aware that one scrubs one's hands in any ritual handwashing?
¹⁷⁹ again, דבר חוצץ. Such as a ring that would prevent the water from coming into contact with the skin.

ויגביהם בנטילה שלא יחזרו מים הראשונים ויטמאו את הידים.


He should raise them in the washing so that the first waters do not drip down¹⁸⁰ and ritually impurify his hands.

¹⁸⁰ יחזרו

Then let him dry them very well.

ויברך על הלחם המוציא. ויתן ריוח בין תיבה לתיבה.


Now he blesses *hamotzi* over the bread, but he must leave an interval between each and every letter.¹⁸¹ in לחם מין into one another.

footnotes:
¹⁸¹ Lest he run the two letters mem



## Paragraph 122

###### Sefer HaYirah 122

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/122](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/122)


וכל מה שיהנה בו מן העולם הזה בין בפירות בין בריח בין באכילה בין בכל דבר שיהנה אסור להנות בלא ברכה. ויברך בכל דבר לפניו ולאחריו הברכה האמורה בה על פי רבותינו ע"ה.


And everything from this world that he may enjoy, whether in fruits¹⁸² or in scent or in eating or any other thing that he may enjoy, it is forbidden to enjoy without a blessing. And let him bless every thing with the blessing that was said by our Rabbis before it and after it, may peace be upon them.

footnotes:
¹⁸² פירות



## Paragraph 123

###### Sefer HaYirah 123

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/123](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/123)


ואם אינו בקי ילך אצל בקי ללמוד ואל ישים עצמו כבהמה. ואם לא מצא בקי ילך ויניח הנאתו. ולא יהנה מן הדבר עד שידע לברך עליו את בוראו כדת ומשפט.


Now, if he has no expert [in blessings] let him go to an expert to learn and not make himself like a [dumb] animal.¹⁸³ eats and smells without saying a blessing. See below. But if he does not find an expert he goes and leaves his enjoyment of it. And he shall not enjoy any thing until he knows how to bless its Creator over it according to the law.¹⁸⁴

footnotes:
¹⁸³ In case he does not know the proper blessing. A beheimah
¹⁸⁴ כדת וכמשפט.



## Paragraph 124

###### Sefer HaYirah 124

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/124](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/124)


וכשיברך יכוין לבו לברך את הבורא. ולא כדבר הרגיל בפה אדם לצאת שלא בכוונה. וכן בברכת המצוה וכל מעשה האדם אשר יעשה [יעשה] דברים לשם פעלם. ולא מצות אנשים מלומדה מנהג אבות.


Now when he blesses let him direct his heart to bless the Creator and not as a thing that is a habit in the mouth of a man to come out without [proper] intention. And so too with the blessing over a mitzvah.¹⁸⁵ And any deed that a person may do, let him do¹⁸⁶ things for the sake of doing them¹⁸⁷ and not as a commandment trained by people and an ancestral custom.¹⁸⁸. R. Yonah returns to this theme again and again.

footnotes:
¹⁸⁵ e.g., anything that one would say אשר קידשנו במצוותיו.
¹⁸⁶ Vilna, Moriah: [יעשה] יעשה
¹⁸⁷ לְשֵם פֹּעֲלָם
¹⁸⁸ A frequent trap of ritual observance is doing things by rote and by memory, without giving them proper kavvanah



## Paragraph 125

###### Sefer HaYirah 125

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/125](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/125)


ומאד יהיה צנוע באכילתו


He should have excellent manners when he eats.



## Paragraph 126

###### Sefer HaYirah 126

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/126](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/126)


[ואחר] גמר סעודתו יברך ברכת המזון.


And after he finishes¹⁸⁹ his meal he should recite the blessing of the food.¹⁹⁰.

footnotes:
¹⁸⁹ Vilna, גמר  [ואחר] "[and after] he finishes"; Moriah: בַּאֲכִילָתוֹ, גָמַר ("his eating...he finishes...").
¹⁹⁰ The grace after meals, Birkat ha-Mazon



## Paragraph 127

###### Sefer HaYirah 127

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/127](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/127)


ואחר כך ישתה מים או יין להשלים מאה ברכות ביום.


Afterwards, let him drink water or wine to complete one hundred blessings in a day.¹⁹¹

footnotes:
¹⁹¹ It is a worthy goal to recite one hundred blessings in a day, so he should drink a little water or wine just for the sake of achieving the goal.



## Paragraph 128

###### Sefer HaYirah 128

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/128](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/128)


והכל לשם שמים:


And all [of these blessings] for the sake of heaven.¹⁹². It is not enough just to recite 100 blessings; one should actually mean them.

footnotes:
¹⁹² See the earlier discussions in this work about proper kavvanah



## Paragraph 129

###### Sefer HaYirah 129

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/129](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/129)


וטרם ילך לעסקיו ילמוד פסוק או הלכה לקיים מצות והגית בו (יהושע א).


**Now, before**¹⁹³ he goes to his work, let him learn one verse [of Torah] or one law to fulfill the mitzvah of "you shall meditate on it day and night." (Josh. 1:8).

footnotes:
¹⁹³ Boldface in Vilna as well as Moriah, Me'ah She'arim.



## Paragraph 130

###### Sefer HaYirah 130

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/130](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/130)


ואותה שעה ילמוד בהגיון הלב. ולהיות אזניו ועיניו פקוחות אל אשר מוציא בשפתיו.


At the same time let him learn with musing of the heart. And let his ears and his eyes be open to what his lips bring forth.



## Paragraph 131

###### Sefer HaYirah 131

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/131](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/131)


ואל יפסיק משנתו לדברים בטלים. כי אף לומר מה נאה אילן זה מה נאה ניר זה אשר יש בו דבר שבח שמברכין עליהן ברוך שככה לו בעולמו הרי זה מתחייב בנפשו. ק"ו לשיחת חולין אשר כל דברי עבירה תלויין בה.


Let him not break off from his study for idle things. For even saying, "How beautiful is this tree," or "How beautiful is that field," in which there is a excellent thing¹⁹⁴ such that one would recite, "Blessed is the One who has such in His world," over it, why, this one is liable for the death-penalty.¹⁹⁵ (Avot, 3:7)¹⁹⁶ How much the more so for profane chatter, upon which all words of sin hang.

footnotes:
¹⁹⁴ דבר שבח
¹⁹⁵ חייב בנפשו. Olitzky's Avot translation renders, "sins against his soul," but this is an interpretive translation.
¹⁹⁶ Moriah and Me'ah She'arim cite to Avot 3:9, but this appears to be a variant ed. Chanoch Albeck numbers the mishnah 3:7.



## Paragraph 132

###### Sefer HaYirah 132

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/132](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/132)


והשח בהן עובר בלאו ועשה. עשה. ודברת בם (דברים ו) לא בדברים אחרים ולאו הבא מכלל עשה עשה. ולאו. כל הדברים יגעים לא יוכל איש לדבר (קהלת א). וכל שכן להפסיק מדברי תורה (החייבין) [אשר הוא חייב] לעסוק בהן. ועל המפסיקין נאמר (איוב ל) הקוטפים מלוח עלי שיח ושורש רתמים לחמם [ואמרו רבותינו ז"ל כל הפוסקים מדברי תורה ועוסקים בדברי שיחה] מאכילים אותם גחלי רתמים בגיהנם.


And one who chatters on them transgresses both a negative and a positive commandment.¹⁹⁷ The positive commandment to "speak upon them,"¹⁹⁸ prayer, "...teach them faithfully to your children, and speak of them when you lie down and when you rise up..." (Deut. 6:7) not of other things. And the negative [commandment] comes from the positive [commandment]: what it says to do, do [and what it says not to do, do not do].¹⁹⁹ And the negative, "all such things are wearisome; no man can ever state them" (Ecclesiastes, 1:8).²⁰⁰ Moreover, everyone who does break off from words of Torah are obligated²⁰¹ to engage in them. And about those who break off [from Torah] it is said, "They cut up mallows by the bushes, and roots of broom for warmth" (Job, 30:4).²⁰² And our sages of blessed memory said, all those who break off from words of Torah to engage in idle chatter,²⁰³ They feed them fiery coals in *Geihinnom*.²⁰⁴

footnotes:
¹⁹⁷ BT, Yoma, 19b.
¹⁹⁸ That is, words of Torah. This is a quotation from the Torah portion that contains ve'ahavta
¹⁹⁹ ולאו הבא מכלל עשה עשה
²⁰⁰ Translation following JPS.
²⁰¹ Vilna: [אשר הוא חייב] (החייבין). Zilber ed.: החייבין. Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: שֶהוּא חַיָּב, "since he is obligated."
²⁰² Translation following Koren. JPS: "They pluck saltwort and wormwood; the roots of broom are their food." The prooftext works by the connection of the word שיח in its Biblical sense of a plant with שיח in its Rabbinic sense of idle chatter. The difference is in how they translate לחמם. Koren renders "ל-חם-ם," "for their warmth"; JPS renders "לחם-ם," "their food."
²⁰³ Vilna places [ואמרו רבותינו ז"ל...שיח] in square brackets; Zilber ed. omits the clause. Moriah and Me'ah She'arim include it without brackets.
²⁰⁴ Hell. BT, Chagigah, 12b.



## Paragraph 133

###### Sefer HaYirah 133

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/133](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/133)


אחרי כן ילך לעסקיו כי יפה דרך ארץ ולא יוכל אדם לעבוד הבורא אם לא יטרח לבקש מזונותיו. וכן אמר דוד (תהלים קכח) אשרי כל ירא ה' ההולך בדרכיו יגיע כפיך כי תאכל אשריך וטוב לך.


**After**²⁰⁵ this, he should go to his business, for it is good to earn a living:²⁰⁶ no one can serve the Creator if he is busy begging for his meals.²⁰⁷ And thus said [King] David: "Happy is everyone who fears the LORD; who walks in his ways. For you shall eat the labor of your hands." (Ps. 128:1-2).²⁰⁸

footnotes:

or an assembly of scoffers.²¹⁰ "an assembly of scoffers." But if he must sit with them: in case they mock and make light of him²¹¹ 21b. and he should appear as one who puffs himself up²¹² let him not interrupt their words or reply²¹³ to them except with shortness of breath²¹⁴ and let him get up as immediately as he is able.

## Paragraph 152

###### Sefer HaYirah 152

ואפילו אם יהיו לו אלף עבדים יטרח הוא בעצמו. ויעמוד עליהם. מי לנו גדול כאברהם ששמש למלאכים והיו לו כמה עבדים ושפחות שנאמר (ובני ביתו) [אולי צ"ל ואת כל ילידי ביתו וגו' (בראשית יז)] אף על פי שנראו כערביים כדברי רבותינו ז"ל. ובכל מה שעשה על ידי עצמו שלם לו הקב"ה לישראל בכבודו ובעצמו.


And even if he may have a thousand servants, he should serve them himself. Who do we have as great as Abraham who served the messengers [of the Lord]²³⁶ but had many manservants and maidservants, as it is said, "and all his homeborn slaves, and all those he had bought" (Gen. 17:23).²³⁷ And despite that they appeared like Arabs²³⁸ according to our Rabbis of blessed memory:²³⁹ 86b and with everything that Abraham did by himself, the Holy One Blessed Be He repaid on his behalf to Israel in His dignity and Himself.²⁴⁰

Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: וְאֵת כָּל-יְלִדֵי בֵיתוֹ (בראשית יז כג) . Context: after Sarai's name change to Sarah, Abraham circumcises his entire household, both those born in his household and all its members. Translation follows JPS.

²⁴⁰ ובכל מה שעשה על ידי עצמו שלם לו הקב"ה לישראל בכבודו ובעצמו.

###### Sefer HaYirah 153

He should not recount before them, "such and such happened²⁴¹ to me," from some of his troubles,²⁴² for these²⁴³ words shatter their spirits, appearing to them that for their sake he says so and that he hardly has any money in his purse.²⁴⁴

But let him honor them as his lords. And so we find with Abraham, who called them "my lords," (Gen. 18:3). And greater was welcoming them than receiving the face of the Shekhinah.²⁴⁵

²⁴⁵ Welcoming guests is one of the duties whose reward is beyond measure. M. Pe'ah 1:1, BT Shabbat 127.



## Paragraph 155

###### Sefer HaYirah 155

ובשעת האוכל יראה את עצמו כמצטער שאינו יכול להיטיב להם יותר. וכן הוא אומר (ישעיה נח) ותפק לרעב נפשך. תצא נפשי שאין לי מה ליתן לך.


And at meal-time, he should show himself as one who grieves ²⁴⁶that he is not able to prepare more for them:²⁴⁷ And so it says,²⁴⁸ "if you draw out your soul to the hungry," (Isa. 58:10).²⁴⁹ Therefore say to him²⁵⁰ "I am dying that I have no more to give you."²⁵¹

## Paragraph 156

###### Sefer HaYirah 156

ואם ילינו אצלו ישכיבם במיטב מטותיו. כי גדולה מנוחת העיף בהיותו שוכב בטוב. ויותר עושה לו נחת רוח המשכיבו היטב יותר מן המאכילו ומשקהו וגלגל הוא החוזר בעולם.


So if they should stay with him, he should lay them down in the best of his beds, for it is greater to rest the weary than to be well-rested.²⁵² And giving [his guest] rest makes more rest²⁵³ for him than to feed him or slake his thirst.²⁵⁴ And it comes back to him in the next world.²⁵⁵

²⁵³ נחת רוח. or satisfaction, gratification, pleasure.

He should give them a bit of provision for the road²⁵⁶, for "a man will transgress for a piece of bread," (Prov. 28:21).²⁵⁷ For since Jonathan did not give David a piece of bread when he parted from him the thing caused the priests of Nov to be killed²⁵⁸ 104a. The statement refers to the episode of David and Jonathan. See 1 Sam. 20:1-22:19., eighty-five men who wore the linen Ephod.²⁵⁹ And you shall surely press [gifts] upon them according to the donation of your hand.²⁶⁰

footnotes:
²⁵⁶ לדרך  Exactly as we say, "for the road"!
²⁵⁷ Translation following Koren.
²⁵⁸ .נתגלגֵל הדבר ונהרגו כהני נוב Perhaps, "the matter spiraled out of control and the Priests of Nov were killed." BT, Sanhedrin.

²⁶⁰ והעניק תעניק להם מסת נדבת ידך.



## Paragraph 159

###### Sefer HaYirah 159

ואם בא מצוה לידך (שמש) [תמהר] בה ואל תתרשל בה. כי זהירות מביא לידי זריזות נקיות פרישות טהרה קדושה יראת חטא חסידות וענוה.


**Now, if**²⁶¹ you have the opportunity to do a *mitzvah*²⁶² make haste²⁶³ to do it and do not be relaxed about it.²⁶⁴ For caution brings one into zeal, cleanliness, separation, purity, holiness, fear of sin, piety, and humility.²⁶⁵

²⁶² ואם באה מצוה לידך., Lit., "if a mitzvah comes into your hand." Note the shift to second person.
²⁶³ Vilna: [תמהר] (שמש) "(serve)[hurry]." Zilber ed.: שמש. Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: תְּמַהֵר.
²⁶⁴ Makor ha-Yirah that R. Yonah is putting two halachot together: 1) to do the mitzvah by oneself not by an appointee, and 2) being rapid about it.
²⁶⁵ BT Avodah Zarah 20b.



## Paragraph 160

###### Sefer HaYirah 160

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/160](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/160)

²⁶⁶ Boldface in Moriah.



## Paragraph 161

###### Sefer HaYirah 161

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/161](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/161)


יש חתן בעיר לך ועסוק בו ושמחהו. כי המשמחו זוכה לראות בשמחת ירושלים.


If there is a groom in the city, go outfit him²⁶⁷ and rejoice with him, for one who rejoices with him merits seeing the redemption of Jerusalem.²⁶⁸

footnotes:
²⁶⁷ I.e., make sure he has enough money to put on a proper wedding.
²⁶⁸ BT, Ketubot 17a.



## Paragraph 162

###### Sefer HaYirah 162

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/162](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/162)


יש מת בעיר טרח בו וספדהו. אך במדה בינונית. כי אין כבודו לומר מה שלא היה בו. ושתיקה יפה. אך המדות אשר ידעת אשר היו בו טובות תספר ותלוהו.


If there is a death in the city, attend to the dead²⁶⁹ and mourn him. Even in a middling aspect²⁷⁰ for it does not honor him²⁷¹ to tell beyond what he had, and silence is beautiful. Only those good characteristics which you know that he had you shall tell and you shall escort him.²⁷²

footnotes:
²⁶⁹ I.e., perform שמירה וטהרה, the rituals of washing the dead body and standing guard over it for the entire period before burial.
²⁷⁰ I.e., Engage in the preparation and eulogize, but do not aggrandize the dead.
²⁷¹ Vilna reads און, sin, but it appears to be an error.
²⁷² Referring to the actual funeral service and burial.



## Paragraph 163

###### Sefer HaYirah 163

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/163](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/163)


אם יש ברית מילה בעיר טרח ועסוק והיה כמסייע במצוה. כי גדול שכר מסייע כעושה מצוה.


If there is a circumcision [to be done]²⁷³ in the city, attend to him²⁷⁴ and be as one who assists in the *mitzvah*,²⁷⁵ 13b on one who does not return מיסב the mitzvah of his friend for the reward of the one who assists is as great as of the one who does the *mitzvah*.²⁷⁶ 5b.

footnotes:
²⁷³ i.e., not only the circumcision itself, but the feast that should take place afterwards.
²⁷⁴ טרח ועֲסוק בו
²⁷⁵ Zilber: See BT Pesachim
²⁷⁶ Zilber: BT. Makkot

ואם חבריך בצרה היה צר בצרתם.


If your fellows are in distress be distressed in their distress.²⁷⁷

Do not rejoice in their downfall, but be happy at their good fortune.²⁷⁸. Zilber, BT *Ta'anit* 20b. M. *Avot*, 4:12 (let the honor of your fellow be as the honor of your teacher).

Moreover, if you have heard of the distress of Jews²⁷⁹ far away, groan and pray²⁸⁰ for them,²⁸¹ 12b, *Ta'anit* 21b, *Yevamot* 52b; *Rambam* ch. 1 of the laws of fasting הל"א = halacha 1 or halacha 31. and just as much for those near by.²⁸² 52b.

footnotes:
²⁷⁹ ישראל
²⁸⁰ הֵאָנַח ותתפלל. Vilna: האָנח.

²⁸² Zilber: "just as much for those near by" BT Yevamot



## Paragraph 167

###### Sefer HaYirah 167

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/167](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Sefer%20HaYirah/r/167)


ברך הצור על כל הבא על הטוב ועל הרע. תשמח ביסורין וחשוב הכל לטובה בכל מדה ומדה שהוא מודד לך וזהו (דברים ו) בכל מאודך. ואל תבעט ביסורין. וחשוב הכל לטובה. ואמור כל דעביד רחמנא לטב.


Bless the Rock²⁸³ [of Israel] over everything that comes, over the good and over the bad, rejoice in suffering²⁸⁴ and consider everything for good, with each and every measure that He measures out for you. And this is²⁸⁵ "and with all your might." (Deut. 6:5).²⁸⁶, "You shall love the LORD your God with all your heart, and with all your soul, and with all your might." And do not be contumacious in suffering,²⁸⁷ but consider²⁸⁸ it all for the good, and say, "Whatever the Merciful One does is for good."²⁸⁹ 60b. Translation following Jastrow. Zilber: BT *Ta'anit* 21a. Vilna, Zilber ed.: כל דעביד רחמנא לטב. Moriah: כָּל דְּעָבֵד רַחֲמָנָא -- לְטוֹב. Me'ah She'arim: כֹּל דְּעָבִיד רַחֲמָנָא לְטַב עָבִיד.
Makor ha-Yirah: Berachot 54a, 60b. "Rejoice in suffering," BT Shabbat 85b. "And do not rebel..." TY, Shekalim, 8:4 (פ"ח ה"ד). "Consider it all for the good..." BT Berachot 60b. Shulchan Aruch, Orach Chayyim § 222. "Whatever the Merciful One does..." BT Ta'anit 21a.

footnotes:
²⁸³ That is, "the Rock of Israel," an appellation for God, as in the popular Chanukah hymn, מאוז צור. Especially as in צדוק הדין, the acceptance of judgment recited upon a death which repeatedly refers to God as הצור, “the Rock.” Vilna, Zilber ed.: ברך הצור. Moriah, Me'ah She'arim: בָּרֵךְ אֶת הַצּוּר
²⁸⁴ Vilna, Moriah: ביסורין (Rabbinic plural). Zilber ed., Me'ah She'arim: ביסורים (Biblical plural).
²⁸⁵ Vilna, Zilber ed., Moriah, וזהו בכל-מאדך. Me'ah She'arim: וְזֶהוּ שֶׁכָּתוּב (דברים ו ה) וּבְכָל-מְאֹדֶךָ
²⁸⁶ As in the ve'ahavta
²⁸⁷ Zilber ed. omits this phrase from ואל תבעט through לטובה. Moriah: וְאַל תְּבַעֵט בְּיִסּוּרִין. pointing the verb in the binyan piel, "do not be contumacious" (that is rebellious, challenging instead of accepting). Me'ah She'arim: ואֵל תִּבְעַט בְּיִסּוּרִים. pointing the verb in the qal, "do not resist, or reject."
²⁸⁸ Me'ah She'arim: וְתַחְשׁוֹב qal imperfect; others וַחֲשוב, qal imperative.
²⁸⁹ Aramaic. See BT Berachot



## Paragraph 168

והתרחק מן הנדרים. כי בעון הנדרים בנים מתים ואשתו של אדם מתה.


**Keep away**²⁹⁰ from vows, for on account of broken vows children die and a man's wife dies.²⁹¹ 32b, *Nedarim* 20a. Rabbinic Judaism has long been skeptical of taking vows.

footnotes:
²⁹⁰ Boldface in Moriah, Me'ah She'arim.
²⁹¹ Back to third person. Makor ha-Yirah: BT Shabbat



## Paragraph 169
